<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> There are absolute constants <i>ε</i> &gt; 0 and <i>C</i> such that every sufficiently large even <i>n</i>-point set in the plane with no three collinear has at most $`Cn^{4/3-\varepsilon}`$ unordered halving pairs. This gives a power saving over the classical $`O(n^{4/3})`$ bound for planar halving lines. The proof is nonquantitative and does not supply explicit constants.

- **claim-halving-power** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-halving-power)): There exist absolute constants $\varepsilon>0$, $C<\infty$, and $n_0$ such that, for every even $n\ge n_0$ and every $n$-point set $P\subset\mathbb R^2$ with no three collinear, $$h(P)\le C n^{4/3-\varepsilon}.$$
  - Explanation: For large even sets in the plane with no three collinear, the number of halving pairs is bounded by a constant times a power of the size strictly below the classical four-thirds exponent. The proof is nonquantitative, so no value of the saving or of the constants is given.
- **claim-all-ranks** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-all-ranks)): There are absolute constants $\varepsilon>0$ and $C<\infty$ such that for every integer $N\ge1$, every generic $N$-point planar configuration $P$, and every integer $0\le k\le N$, $$S_k(P)\le C N^{4/3-\varepsilon}.$$
  - Explanation: The same power saving holds for the number of switches of every rank sweep of a generic configuration, uniformly in the rank. Uniformity is needed because restricting to participating points changes the rank.
- **claim-k-sensitive** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-k-sensitive)): There are absolute constants $\varepsilon_0>0$ and $C_0<\infty$ such that, for every integer $n\ge2$ and every integer $1\le k\le n/2$, the number of $k$-sets of any $n$-point set $Q\subset\mathbb R^2$ with no three collinear is at most $$\label{eq:k-sensitive} C_0 n(k+1)^{1/3-\varepsilon_0}.$$ The same bound holds for $\operatorname{comp}(\Lambda_k(\mathcal L))$ for any arrangement $\mathcal L$ of $n$ nonvertical straight lines with no parallel pair and no three concurrent.
  - Explanation: Applying the uniform sweep bound inside shallow-cutting cells gives a power saving in the rank parameter, for planar k-sets and for levels of arrangements of straight lines.
- **claim-activity-packing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-activity-packing)): For every $\xi>0$ there is $Q_\xi<\infty$ such that, for every generic $N$-point configuration, every rank, and every integer $1\le b\le N$ with $N/b\ge Q_\xi$, any collection of intervals with disjoint step sets and activity at least $b$ has at most $\xi(N/b)^{4/3}$ members.
  - Explanation: The number of step-disjoint intervals of large activity is below any prescribed small multiple of the classical crossing bound once the ratio of size to activity is large enough, uniformly in the rank.
- **lemma-activity-distance** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-activity-distance)): Fix a generic $N$-point set and a rank $k$. For an integer $b\ge4$, any collection of intervals of activity at least $b$ can be divided into two classes. At rank $k+\lfloor b/4\rfloor$ the intervals in the first class have endpoint distance at least $\lfloor b/4\rfloor$; at rank $k-\lfloor b/4\rfloor$ the intervals in the second class do so. Each rank used by a nonempty class is feasible. For $1\le b<4$, the original rank already gives endpoint distance at least one.
  - Explanation: Intervals of large activity split into two classes, each having large endpoint distance at a suitably shifted rank.
- **lemma-greedy-batches** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-greedy-batches)): Fix a rank and an integer $d\ge1$, and put $a=\lceil d/2\rceil$. Starting at the first state, end a batch at the first subsequent state at distance $a$ from its start, and repeat while possible. If $R$ complete batches are obtained, every collection of intervals with disjoint step sets and endpoint distance at least $d$ has at most $R$ members.
  - Explanation: A greedy cut of the sweep into batches of fixed endpoint distance bounds the size of any disjoint family of intervals with large endpoint distance by the number of batches.
- **lemma-chain-partitions** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-chain-partitions)): For a consecutive window of batches, let $S$ be the number of distinct endpoints of its exchange edges. Those edges partition into at most $aS$ convex polygonal chains, and also into at most $aS$ concave polygonal chains. The number of properly crossing edge pairs is at most $2a^2S^2$.
  - Explanation: The exchange edges of a window of batches split into few convex chains and few concave chains, and a convex and a concave chain cross only a bounded number of times, which bounds all proper crossings.
- **lemma-designated-crossing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-designated-crossing)): Let a simple graph be drawn with straight segments on $v$ distinct points, with no vertex in an edge interior and no crossings of incident edges. Suppose every edge has an endpoint in a specified set of at most $m\le v$ vertices. If the graph has $e$ edges, then $$e^3\le 2304\,mv\,\operatorname{cr}(G)+13824\,v^3.$$ In particular, if $e\ge24v$, then $e^3\le2304\,mv\,\operatorname{cr}(G)$.
  - Explanation: A crossing inequality for straight-line graphs in which every edge has an endpoint in a small designated vertex set, proved by random sampling of vertices.
- **lemma-edge-vertex-mass** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-edge-vertex-mass)): For every window $J$ and every Borel set $F\subset Z$, $$\label{eq:local-mass} \lambda_J(F)^3\le C s_J^3\mu_J(F).$$ In particular $\lambda\ll\mu$. The same estimate holds if additional labels in a compact metrizable space are attached to vertices, inherited by their edge endpoints, and a further weak limit is taken.
  - Explanation: In the limit, edge endpoint mass in a window is controlled cubically by participating vertex mass, so edge mass is absolutely continuous with respect to vertex mass.
- **lemma-nonatomic-time** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-nonatomic-time)): For $\lambda$-almost every $z$, the probability measure $h_z$ is nonatomic.
  - Explanation: For almost every label the conditional distribution of batch time has no atoms.
- **lemma-edge-crossing-ac** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-edge-crossing-ac)): For every window $K$, $$\label{eq:crossing-ac} \lambda_K\ll\sum_{I,J\subset K,\ I\cap J=\varnothing}\sigma_{IJ},$$ where the sum specifies null sets. Thus, if a Borel set $F\subset Z$ is null for every $\sigma_{IJ}$ with $I,J\subset K$ disjoint windows, then $\lambda_K(F)=0$.
  - Explanation: Any positive portion of limiting edge mass in a window is detected by crossing measures of disjoint subwindows.
- **lemma-monotone-differentiability** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-monotone-differentiability)): A bounded Borel function on an open rectangle in $\mathbb R^2$ that is monotone in each coordinate is Fréchet differentiable almost everywhere with respect to planar Lebesgue measure.
  - Explanation: Coordinatewise monotone bounded functions of two variables are differentiable almost everywhere, shown by reduction to Lipschitz pieces and Rademacher's theorem.
- **lemma-critical-image-null** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-critical-image-null)): Let $F:\Omega\to\mathbb R^2$ be any map, where $\Omega\subset\mathbb R^2$ is open. The image of the set of points at which $F$ is Fréchet differentiable and $\operatorname{rank}DF<2$ has planar outer measure zero.
  - Explanation: The image of the set where a planar map is differentiable with singular derivative has planar outer measure zero, proved by a covering argument.
- **lemma-independent-gradients** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-independent-gradients)): After discarding a further $\lambda\|_E$-null set, every label $z\in E$ has the following property: if $J\subset K$ is dyadic and $h_z(J)>0$, then some disjoint dyadic $C,D\subset J$ satisfy $$\det\begin{pmatrix}\nabla f_C(p)\\ \nabla f_D(p)\end{pmatrix}\ne0.$$
  - Explanation: Almost every label has, in each positively active window, two disjoint subwindows whose coordinate gradients are linearly independent.
- **lemma-separated-tangent-directions** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-separated-tangent-directions)): If $s<t$ lie in $K^\circ$ and $h_z((s,t))>0$, then $D_p(s)\cap D_p(t)=\varnothing$. There are three times $\tau_1<\tau_2<\tau_3$ in $K^\circ$ for which these intervals are singletons with distinct projective directions.
  - Explanation: Allowed tangent direction sets at two times separated by positive conditional time mass are disjoint, and three times can be found whose directions are distinct singletons.
- **lemma-three-foliation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-three-foliation)): Let $\Omega_j\subset\mathbb R^2$ be open sets containing every compact subset of $\mathbb R^2$ for all sufficiently large $j$. Let $F_j:\mathbb R^2\longrightarrow\Omega_j$ be homeomorphisms. Put $v_1=(1,0)$, $v_2=(0,1)$ and $v_3=(1,1)$. Suppose the following hold.* 1. *There is a fixed invertible matrix $P$ such that each coordinate of $P F_j$ is monotone on each line parallel to $v_i$. For each $i$, the two monotonicity signs are independent of the line and of $j$.* 2. *Along every subsequence, if $x_j,y_j$ lie on one line parallel to $v_i$ and $F_j(x_j)\to a$, $F_j(y_j)\to b$, then $b-a\in\mathbb R v_i$. The points and their common line may depend on $j$.* 3. *$F_j(0)=0$ and $\|F_j(v_1)\|=1$.* *After passage to a subsequence there is $\sigma\in\{-1,1\}$ such that $F_j\to \sigma\operatorname{id}$ uniformly on compact sets. The inverse maps converge to $\sigma\operatorname{id}$ uniformly on compact sets as well.
  - Explanation: Homeomorphisms that respect coordinate monotonicity along three transverse line families and a normalization converge, after a subsequence, to a signed identity, together with their inverses.
- **prop-spatial-limit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-spatial-limit)): There is a Borel set $G\subset E$ with $\lambda_{K_0}(G)>0$, a further subsequence, and a translation and uniform rescaling of each normalized Euclidean plane with the following properties. Over every $p\in\pi(G)$ the limiting graph of the maps $L_n$ has a unique position $l(p)$ in the closed unit disk. With $\eta=1/16$, there is $c_G>0$ such that $$\|l(p)-l(q)\|\ge c_G\|p-q\|^{1+\eta} \qquad(p,q\in\pi(G)). \label{eq:inverse-holder}$$ Attach Euclidean position as an additional compactified coordinate to the vertex measures, and take a weak limit $\widetilde\mu$. Its restriction by the old-label condition $z\in G$ has position marginal $\nu_G$ satisfying $$\nu_G(B(x,r))\le C_G r^{d_*}, \qquad d_*=\frac{2}{1+\eta}=\frac{32}{17}, \qquad x\in\mathbb R^2,\quad 0<r<1. \label{eq:disk-bound}$$ Consequently every strip of width $r$ has $\nu_G$-mass at most $C'_G r^{d_*-1}$, uniformly in its position and direction. Every line has zero $\nu_G$-mass.
  - Explanation: A positive portion of edge mass admits a single-valued Euclidean limit with an inverse Hölder bound, which yields disk and strip mass bounds for the vertex measure.
- **lemma-three-strips** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-three-strips)): Let $P$ be a finite subset of $\mathbb R^2$, let $B$ be a bounded open set, and let $D\geq1$ satisfy $\|x\|\leq D$ for $x\in B$. Suppose that $M_0,\ldots,M_m\subset P$ have the same cardinality and are given by strictly separating cuts $$M_i=\{x\in P:\nu_i\cdot x<c_i\}, \qquad \|\nu_i\|=1, \qquad \|\nu_i-\nu_0\|\leq\theta.$$ For each $i\geq1$, choose a bijection from $M_{i-1}\setminus M_i$ to $M_i\setminus M_{i-1}$. Let $C_B$ count the resulting transfers having exactly one endpoint in $B$, and let $$A=\bigcup_{i=1}^m(M_{i-1}\mathbin\triangle M_i)$$ be the set of distinct participating vertices. Set $$\rho=D\theta,\qquad c_- =\min_{0\leq i\leq m}c_i, \qquad c_+=\max_{0\leq i\leq m}c_i,$$ and, for $q\in\{c_0,c_-,c_+\}$, define the closed strip $$S_q=\{x\in\mathbb R^2:\|\nu_0\cdot x-q\|\leq3\rho\}.$$ Then $$\#\bigl((A\cap B)\setminus(S_{c_0}\cup S_{c_-}\cup S_{c_+})\bigr) \leq2C_B,$$ and consequently $$\#(A\cap B) \leq 2C_B+ \sum_{q\in\{c_0,c_-,c_+\}}\#(P\cap B\cap S_q). \label{eq:finite-three-strips}$$ Repeated thresholds may be counted repeatedly in the sum.
  - Explanation: Participating vertices in a bounded region either lie in three narrow strips or are accounted for by tokens crossing the region boundary.
- **lemma-limiting-boundary-activity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-limiting-boundary-activity)): For each dyadic $J\subset K_0$, $$\mu_J(G)\leq2b_J+C_G\theta_J^{d-1}. \label{eq:limiting-boundary-activity}$$
  - Explanation: In the limit the participating vertex mass on good labels in a window is bounded by boundary transfers plus a power of the angular span.
- **prop-packing-to-power** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-packing-to-power)): Suppose that, for every $\eta>0$, there is $R_\eta<\infty$ such that for every generic planar configuration of $N$ points, every rank, and every integer $1\le b\le N$ with $N/b\ge R_\eta$, the number of step-disjoint intervals of activity at least $b$ is at most $\eta(N/b)^{4/3}$. Then there are absolute constants $\varepsilon>0$ and $C$ such that every such rank sweep has at most $CN^{4/3-\varepsilon}$ switches.
  - Explanation: If the activity-packing bound holds with arbitrarily small constant for large size-to-activity ratio, a fixed-scale recurrence yields a power saving for all rank sweeps.

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-activity-distance",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a generic $N$-point set and a rank $k$. For an integer $b\\ge4$, any collection of intervals of activity at least $b$ can be divided into two classes. At rank $k+\\lfloor b/4\\rfloor$ the intervals in the first class have endpoint distance at least $\\lfloor b/4\\rfloor$; at rank $k-\\lfloor b/4\\rfloor$ the intervals in the second class do so. Each rank used by a nonempty class is feasible. For $1\\le b<4$, the original rank already gives endpoint distance at least one.",
        "source_id": "PM-183.1"
      },

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **halving pair** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A pair of points is a *halving pair* if its line leaves exactly $(n-2)/2$ other points in each open halfplane.
- **k-set** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A *$k$-set* of a planar point configuration is a $k$-point subset strictly separable from the remaining points by a line.
- **switch** ([source](#sec:finite)): The latter kind of step is a *switch*.
- **activity** ([source](#sec:finite)): Its *activity* is the number of distinct points whose membership in $M_k$ changes at one of those steps.

|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-halving-bound | number of halving pairs h(P) | ≤ | $C n^{4/3-\varepsilon}$ | Absolute constants epsilon, C, n_0 not given numerically |
| q-switch-bound | number of switches S_k(P) | ≤ | $C N^{4/3-\varepsilon}$ | Uniform over every rank |
| q-kset-bound | number of k-sets and level complexity | ≤ | $C_0 n(k+1)^{1/3-\varepsilon_0}$ | Rank range up to half the size |
| q-activity-packing | number of step-disjoint intervals of activity at least b | ≤ | $\xi(N/b)^{4/3}$ | Large size-to-activity ratio |
| q-chain-crossings | properly crossing edge pairs in a window | ≤ | $2a^2S^2$ | Batch parameter a and S distinct endpoints |
| q-designated-crossing | cube of the edge count e | ≤ | $2304\,mv\,\operatorname{cr}(G)+13824\,v^3$ | Designated set of at most m vertices among v |
| q-spatial-dimension | auxiliary spatial exponent d_* | = | $\frac{32}{17}$ | Auxiliary exponent, not the final saving |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-activity-distance** [claim](#stmt-lem-activity-distance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-activity-distance): Classify intervals by whether active points were mostly initially unmarked or marked, then compare with a shifted-rank marked set. -> endpoint distance lower bound at one of two ranks
- **step-greedy-batches** [claim](#stmt-lem-greedy-batches) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-greedy-batches): Cut the sweep greedily into batches of fixed endpoint distance and assign each interval a batch end. -> bound by number of batches
- **step-chain-partitions** [claim](#stmt-lem-chain-partitions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-chain-partitions): Decompose exchanges into matchings and follow tokens to get convex and concave chains; count crossings by chain pairs. -> crossing bound for a window
- **step-designated-crossing** [claim](#stmt-lem-designated-crossing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-designated-crossing): Random bipartite cut, then asymmetric vertex sampling combined with the planar edge bound. -> cubic crossing inequality
- **step-edge-vertex-mass** [claim](#stmt-lem-edge-vertex-mass) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-edge-vertex-mass): Apply the designated-endpoint inequality in finite configurations, then pass to weak limits and use regularity. -> edge mass dominated by vertex mass
- **step-nonatomic-time** [claim](#stmt-lem-nonatomic-time) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-nonatomic-time): Partition tail estimate, Hölder inequality and Fatou's lemma. -> nonatomic conditional time
- **step-edge-crossing-ac** [claim](#stmt-lem-edge-crossing-ac) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-edge-crossing-ac): Discard heavy windows, retain edges near a null set, and bound crossings in the limit. -> edge mass seen by crossing measures
- **step-monotone-differentiability** [claim](#stmt-lem-monotone-differentiability) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-monotone-differentiability): Distributional derivatives are measures; cover by Lipschitz pieces and apply Rademacher's theorem. -> almost-everywhere differentiability
- **step-critical-image-null** [claim](#stmt-lem-critical-image-null) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-critical-image-null): Vitali-type covering with linear approximation at each ball. -> null critical image
- **step-independent-gradients** [claim](#stmt-lem-independent-gradients) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-independent-gradients): Combine critical-image nullity with projection domination and crossing absolute continuity. -> independent gradients on a positive part
- **step-tangent-directions** [claim](#stmt-lem-separated-tangent-directions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-separated-tangent-directions): Independent gradients force disjoint allowed direction sets; countability gives singleton times. -> three distinct tangent directions
- **step-three-foliation** [claim](#stmt-lem-three-foliation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-three-foliation): Converge line images, then intersections on rational grids, then extend by monotonicity and invert via the Jordan curve theorem. -> uniform convergence to a signed identity
- **step-spatial-limit** [claim](#stmt-prop-spatial-limit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-spatial-limit): Fix scale parameters, bound vertex density, take a Hausdorff limit of graphs and derive inverse Hölder and disk bounds. -> disk and strip mass bounds
- **step-three-strips** [claim](#stmt-lem-three-strips) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-three-strips): Compare membership at the extreme threshold states and count by token conservation. -> participants bounded by boundary transfers plus strips
- **step-limiting-boundary** [claim](#stmt-lem-limiting-boundary-activity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-limiting-boundary-activity): Pass the finite three-strip bound to the limit and use the strip mass estimate. -> vertex mass bounded by boundary term
- **step-packing-to-power** [claim](#stmt-prop-packing-to-power) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-packing-to-power): Fix one scale, partition the sweep into few blocks of few active points, and induct on size. -> power saving for rank sweeps
- **step-activity-packing** [claim](#stmt-thm-activity-packing) / [proof](#sec:boundary): Finite reductions give a hypothetical extremal sequence; the boundary estimate contradicts positive limiting edge mass. -> little-o packing bound
- **step-all-ranks** [claim](#stmt-thm-all-ranks) / [proof](#sec:recurrence): Combine activity packing with the recurrence. -> uniform switch bound
- **step-halving-power** [claim](#stmt-thm-halving-power) / [proof](#sec:recurrence): Halving pairs are the middle-rank switches; perturbation reduces to the generic case. -> halving pair bound
- **step-k-sensitive** [claim](#stmt-cor-k-sensitive) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-packing-to-power-2): Duality, shallow cuttings and the all-ranks bound inside each cell. -> k-set and level bound

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-middle-rank-halving**: For a generic configuration of an even number of points, the halving pairs are exactly the switches of the middle-rank sweep.
- **inv-trivial-ranks**: At ranks 0 and N the marked set never changes, so the switch count is zero.
- **inv-one-critical-slope**: Each unordered pair of points has exactly one critical slope in the sweep, so the switches at a rank can be counted pair by pair.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-explicit-constants**: Use the power-saving theorem to fix explicit buffer sizes or thresholds from numeric values of the exponent saving and the constants. -> The proof supplies no numerical saving and no effective constants, so any concrete sizing would be invented.
- **anti-pseudolines**: Apply the level bound to arrangements of pseudolines or to line segments. -> The argument uses straight Euclidean lines and no saving is claimed outside them.
- **anti-collinear-input**: Apply the halving-pair bound to a raw point set containing collinear triples. -> Pairs no longer determine distinct lines and the statement does not cover the input.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `algorithmic`. The rank sweep, its switches and the middle-rank halving pairs are finite objects computed by an explicit procedure from slopes and marked sets; the harness checks the switch count of that procedure on small generic inputs against an independent pairwise count. The asymptotic power saving itself is not tested.

```python
# INPUTS: stdin: N and k on the first line, then N lines with integer x y (generic: distinct x, distinct pairwise slopes)
# PRECONDITIONS: 0 <= k <= N; first coordinates distinct; all determined slopes distinct (hence no three collinear)
# OUTPUTS: one integer, the number of switches S_k(P) of the rank-k slope sweep
# TIME: O(N^2 log N) per state, O(N^2) states, exact rational arithmetic
# SPACE: O(N^2) for the slope list
# ARITHMETIC: exact Fractions over integer inputs
# FAILURE: malformed input raises an exception and a nonzero exit code
import sys
from fractions import Fraction
def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    n = int(data[0]); k = int(data[1])
    pts = [(int(data[2 + 2 * i]), int(data[3 + 2 * i])) for i in range(n)]
    slopes = sorted({Fraction(pts[j][1] - pts[i][1], pts[j][0] - pts[i][0]) for i in range(n) for j in range(i + 1, n)})
    reps = [slopes[0] - 1] if slopes else [Fraction(0)]
    for a, b in zip(slopes, slopes[1:]):
        reps.append((a + b) / 2)
    if slopes: reps.append(slopes[-1] + 1)
    prev = None
    cnt = 0
    for s in reps:
        order = sorted(range(n), key=lambda i: pts[i][1] - s * pts[i][0])
        cur = frozenset(order[:k])
        if prev is not None and cur != prev:
            cnt += 1
        prev = cur
    print(cnt)
main()
```

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result is an asymptotic existence bound with no explicit constants. It supplies no library, API or runtime contract that software could implement. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No simulation, procedural-generation or game mechanic appears. The statement concerns worst-case counts of balanced separating lines for abstract planar point sets. |
| tools | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | The slope-sweep definitions (marked set, switches, middle-rank switches equal halving pairs) can be turned into a small checker that cross-validates a brute-force halving-pair count against a sweep switch count on generic inputs. The theorem itself is not a tool. |
| algorithms | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | The finite reductions (greedy batches, convex and concave chain partitions) are explicit combinatorial procedures that organise a rank sweep. The main improvement is nonconstructive, so no complexity claim follows. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Chain partitions of exchange edges are proof devices for bounding crossings. They are not a maintained structure with update or query operations. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No arithmetic kernel, memory layout or parallel schedule is involved. The argument rests on measure-theoretic limits of abstract configuration sequences. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No hardness assumption, protocol or adversary model appears. Halving-line counts for planar sets carry no security semantics. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No learning task, loss or generalisation statement occurs. The bound counts balanced line separations of finite planar point sets. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No objective is optimised and no relaxation or solver appears. The extremal question bounds a combinatorial count, and the proof uses compactness and contradiction. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No nodes, messages or consensus appear. The tokens that follow marked points are an accounting device in the proof, not a distributed protocol. |
| other | True | moderate | inspirational | stated | The strongest use is as a theoretical reference in discrete and computational geometry. It gives a power saving over the classical exponent for halving pairs, for k-sets, and for levels of straight-line arrangements. The proof template (extremal sequence, weak limits, contradiction) may also inspire other extremal problems. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-nonquantitative-constants** (nonconstructive): The saving epsilon and the constants C and n_0 are not given numerically, so the bound is asymptotic and cannot be evaluated at any concrete size. Blocked use: ['Setting buffer sizes, timeouts or thresholds from the bound', 'Claiming a specific numerical exponent below four thirds', 'Using the bound as a hard assertion on small inputs']
- **limit-general-position** (hypothesis): The halving-pair theorem is stated only for point sets with no three collinear. Blocked use: ['Applying the bound to raw inputs containing collinear triples without perturbing them first']
- **limit-even-and-large** (edge_case): The theorem needs the size to be even and at least the unspecified threshold n_0. Blocked use: ['Applying the bound to odd sizes', 'Applying the bound to small sizes below the threshold']
- **limit-generic-sweep** (hypothesis): The uniform rank-sweep bound is stated for generic configurations, which also require distinct first coordinates and distinct determined slopes. Blocked use: ['Applying the switch bound to configurations with repeated first coordinates or repeated slopes without perturbation']
- **limit-packing-threshold** (formal_scope): The activity packing estimate holds only when the size-to-activity ratio is at least a threshold that depends on the chosen small constant and is not made explicit. Blocked use: ['Using the little-o packing bound for moderate size-to-activity ratios', 'Deriving explicit recurrence scales from the threshold']
- **limit-conditional-recurrence** (nonconstructive): The passage from packing to a power saving takes a scale from a threshold whose existence is only assumed, so it yields no numerical saving. Blocked use: ['Reading a value of epsilon off the recurrence', 'Treating the recurrence as an effective algorithm']
- **limit-lines-only** (counterexample_scope): The k-set and level corollary is for planar point sets and complete straight lines only; no saving is asserted for pseudolines or line segments. Blocked use: ['Bounding levels of pseudoline arrangements', 'Bounding levels of arrangements of line segments']
- **limit-planar-only** (formal_scope): All statements concern points in the plane; the crossing and chain arguments are planar and no higher-dimensional version is claimed. Blocked use: ['Bounding halving hyperplanes of point sets in dimension three or higher']
- **limit-auxiliary-exponents** (approximation): The spatial exponents appearing in the proof are auxiliary and are not the saving in the final bound. Blocked use: ['Identifying the final saving with the spatial exponents']
- **limit-designated-crossing-regime** (hypothesis): The designated-endpoint crossing inequality holds only when every edge has an endpoint in the designated set; the dense-case form needs the edge count large relative to the vertex count. Blocked use: ['Applying the cubic crossing bound to graphs with edges missing the designated set', 'Dropping the sparse term when the edge count is small']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 183 (`lean/docs/183.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [A power saving for planar halving lines](../../preprints/A-power-saving-for-planar-halving-lines-September-25-2026/main.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> A halving pair in an even planar point set is a pair whose line leaves equally many remaining points on each side. The formalization proves that some absolute $\varepsilon>0$ and $C$ bound the number of halving pairs by $Cn^{4/3-\varepsilon}$ for every sufficiently large even $n$ and every $n$-point set with no three collinear. It also proves a bound of the same form for all level-switch counts under the additional generic-position assumptions in the statement. The constants are existential.

| declaration | kind |
|---|---|
| `Point` | abbrev |
| `orient` | def |
| `GeneralPosition` | def |
| `sideCount` | def |
| `halvingCount` | def |
| `slope` | def |
| `Generic` | def |
| `belowCount` | def |
| `switchCount` | def |
| `power_bounds` | theorem |

Comparator source `HalvingLines.lean` (2197 bytes, sha256 `8011f6da81708ed7278237b87810ed246c80fa2669429aee4a4ec69cb8c2d075`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

namespace PlanarHalving

abbrev Point := ℝ × ℝ

def orient (p q r : Point) : ℝ :=
  (q.1 - p.1) * (r.2 - p.2) - (q.2 - p.2) * (r.1 - p.1)

def GeneralPosition {n : ℕ} (P : Fin n → Point) : Prop :=
  Function.Injective P ∧ ∀ i j l, i ≠ j → j ≠ l → i ≠ l →
    orient (P i) (P j) (P l) ≠ 0

noncomputable def sideCount {n : ℕ} (P : Fin n → Point) (i j : Fin n) : ℕ :=
  (Finset.univ.filter fun l => 0 < orient (P i) (P j) (P l)).card

noncomputable def halvingCount {n : ℕ} (P : Fin n → Point) : ℕ :=
  (Finset.univ.filter fun ij : Fin n × Fin n =>
    ij.1 < ij.2 ∧ sideCount P ij.1 ij.2 = (n - 2) / 2 ∧
      sideCount P ij.2 ij.1 = (n - 2) / 2).card

noncomputable def slope (p q : Point) : ℝ := (q.2 - p.2) / (q.1 - p.1)

def Generic {n : ℕ} (P : Fin n → Point) : Prop :=
  GeneralPosition P ∧
  (∀ i j, i ≠ j → (P i).1 ≠ (P j).1) ∧
  (∀ i j a b, i < j → a < b → slope (P i) (P j) = slope (P a) (P b) →
    i = a ∧ j = b) ∧
  (∀ i j a b c d, i < j → a < b → c < d →
    (i, j) ≠ (a, b) → (c, d) ≠ (i, j) → (c, d) ≠ (a, b) →
    ∀ z : Point, z ∈ openSegment ℝ (P i) (P j) →
      z ∈ openSegment ℝ (P a) (P b) → z ∉ segment ℝ (P c) (P d))

noncomputable def belowCount {n : ℕ} (P : Fin n → Point) (i j : Fin n) : ℕ :=
  let s := slope (P i) (P j)
  (Finset.univ.filter fun l => (P l).2 - s * (P l).1 <
    (P i).2 - s * (P i).1).card

noncomputable def switchCount {n : ℕ} (P : Fin n → Point) (k : ℕ) : ℕ :=
  (Finset.univ.filter fun ij : Fin n × Fin n =>
    ij.1 < ij.2 ∧ 0 < k ∧ k < n ∧ belowCount P ij.1 ij.2 = k - 1).card

theorem power_bounds :
    (∃ ε : ℝ, 0 < ε ∧ ∃ C : ℝ, ∃ n₀ : ℕ,
      ∀ n : ℕ, Even n → n₀ ≤ n → ∀ P : Fin n → Point,
        GeneralPosition P → (halvingCount P : ℝ) ≤ C * (n : ℝ) ^ ((4 : ℝ) / 3 - ε)) ∧
    (∃ ε : ℝ, 0 < ε ∧ ∃ C : ℝ,
      ∀ n : ℕ, 1 ≤ n → ∀ P : Fin n → Point, Generic P →
        ∀ k : ℕ, k ≤ n → (switchCount P k : ℝ) ≤ C * (n : ℝ) ^ ((4 : ℝ) / 3 - ε)) :=
  sorry

end PlanarHalving

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


There are absolute constants <i>ε</i> &gt; 0 and <i>C</i> such that every sufficiently large even <i>n</i>-point set in the plane with no three collinear has at most $`Cn^{4/3-\varepsilon}`$ unordered halving pairs. This gives a power saving over the classical $`O(n^{4/3})`$ bound for planar halving lines. The proof is nonquantitative and does not supply explicit constants.
